14:Tc6d,## Summary

The `Validate develop` workflow is red at the current tip `4573712ebf` because the `cloud / agent-stability / deterministic-keyless` lane fails on every attempt, and `Complete manifest` fails with it ("Require one current green row per surface").

Run: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/36636159978 (2026-09-29T21:52Z). The previous run at `2a464d8413` (https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/36628914261) was green; the two runs in between were cancelled by later pushes, so this is the first observed run after the reminder-delivery change landed.

## Failure

The step summary only prints `{"status":"failed","firstAttemptPassed":false,"passedAttempts":0}`. The uploaded `cloud-stability-keyless-1` artifact carries the reason in each attempt's `scenario.stdout.log` (identical for attempts 01-03):

```text
| cloud-stability-agent | failed | 1470ms | deterministic model fixtures were not consumed:
[{"name":"cloud-reminder-dispatch-body","consumed":0,"min":1,"max":1,"required":true},
 {"name":"cloud-reminder-dispatch-title","consumed":0,"min":1,"max":1,"required":true}] |
```

The reminder itself was created and dispatched: the scenario's `assertCloudReadback` guard was never reached because the fixture-consumption check fails first, and the `scenario-report.json` shows `OWNER_REMINDERS_CREATE` with `cadence.kind: "once"`, `metadata.ownerSurface: "OWNER_REMINDERS"`, one `in_app` plan step.

## Cause

`packages/cloud/e2e/scenarios/cloud-stability-agent.scenario.ts` declares two required `TEXT_SMALL` fixtures for the dispatch path:

- `cloud-reminder-dispatch-body` (matches `Current reminder:\n- title: Review the synthetic Cloud inbox`, cardinality 1)
- `cloud-reminder-dispatch-title` (matches `Message body:\nTime to review the synthetic Cloud inbox.`, cardinality 1)

Since `51bd56787c` (fix(reminders): deliver grounded one-shot content without prose rewriting, via #32931 merged as `6d13e67446`), `RemindersService` no longer calls the model for exactly this reminder shape. In `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/lifeops/domains/reminders-service.ts` the `exactReminder` branch (owner occurrence, `in_app`, lifecycle `plan`, `cadence.kind === "once"`, `metadata.ownerSurface === "OWNER_REMINDERS"`, no `bodyOverride`) builds the body with `buildReminderBody` (`Reminder: <title>\nDue: <local time>`) and issues a `ReminderPresentation` whose title is the literal `Reminder`; `renderReminderBody` and `renderOwnerNotificationTitle` are only reached on the non-exact path. The scenario's reminder satisfies every `exactReminder` condition, so neither `TEXT_SMALL` call happens and the two required fixtures stay at `consumed: 0`.

The readback assertion itself still holds with the grounded body (`notification.body.includes("synthetic Cloud inbox")` matches `Reminder: Review the synthetic Cloud inbox`).

## Fix

Remove the two dead fixtures from the deterministic scenario (they encode the pre-`51bd56787c` prose-rewriting contract), leaving the readback assertion to pin the grounded dispatch. Only the deterministic lane is affected; `stability:real` uses the same scenario without fixtures.

I am opening a PR for this.
15:T14aa,**Describe the bug**

`drainCutoverHolds` (`packages/cloud/services/gateway-webhook/src/cutover-hold.ts`) fences record removal to the current lease owner: the Lua step at `:255-258` refuses to delete the hold record when a *different* token owns the lease. That fence is correct while the other owner is alive. It leaves a gap when the other owner dies:

1. Drainer A acquires the lease and starts redelivering a held Blooio or Twilio turn.
2. A's renewal fails for a whole lease period, the lease lapses, and replica B acquires it (`set nx`). B then dies before draining.
3. A's turn completes. The dedup ledger is written `delivered`, but A's `removeHeldRecord()` returns 0 because B's token owns the lease, so the record and its index entry stay.
4. B's lease lapses. The next drainer acquires the lease, finds a live record, and calls `redeliver` without looking at the ledger. The Cloud turn runs again and the provider receives a second send.

This is the "P2" probe from the review of #32909 and the case #32944 left out of scope. #32944 fixed the sibling case where the lease lapsed with *no* new owner; this one needs the drainer to notice that the ledger has already left `held`.

**To Reproduce**

The real `handleWebhook` and `drainCutoverHolds` against the local `Bun.serve` Cloud stub and in-memory Redis used by `__tests__/cutover-hold.test.ts` (Bun 1.3.14, Node 24.15.0, `--conditions=eliza-source`). A signed Blooio webhook is held during a `503 personal_cutover_in_progress`. Cloud is then attested, and while the attested turn runs the lease key is overwritten with another replica's token. After the first drain the lease is deleted (the other replica died and its lease lapsed) and a second drain runs.

On `190d8a3304` (#32944's head) the second drain redelivers:

```
$ bun test --conditions=eliza-source __tests__/cutover-hold.test.ts -t "another replica owned the lease"
534 |     expect(second).toEqual({
error: expect(received).toEqual(expected)
-   "delivered": 0,
+   "delivered": 1,
(fail) connector ingress during Shared→Dedicated cutover > a hold settled while another replica owned the lease is not redelivered once that lease lapses
 0 pass
 1 fail
```

Gateway log for the message (timestamps and trace ids omitted): the turn completes twice and the provider egress runs twice.

```
{"level":"warn","message":"Personal connector turn held for Dedicated cutover","platform":"blooio","messageId":"msg_cutover_8","holdCode":"personal_cutover_in_progress"}
{"level":"info","message":"Personal Eliza connector message completed","platform":"blooio","messageId":"msg_cutover_8","cloudAttempts":1,"egressMs":2,"totalMs":3}
{"level":"info","message":"Held personal connector turn delivered after cutover","platform":"blooio","messageId":"msg_cutover_8","attempts":1}
{"level":"info","message":"Personal Eliza connector message completed","platform":"blooio","messageId":"msg_cutover_8","cloudAttempts":1,"egressMs":1,"totalMs":4}
```

**Expected behavior**

A drainer that acquires the lease over a record whose dedup ledger is no longer `held` does not redeliver. It removes the record under the same owner fence and moves on. The turn above runs once and the provider receives one send.

**Additional context**

The remaining unguarded window is two live drainers running the same turn concurrently, which happens only if renewal fails and a second replica acquires the lease while the first turn is still in flight. Lease renewal (`renewLeaseWhileRedelivering`) exists to prevent that, and the ledger check below cannot see it because the ledger stays `held` until a turn settles.

16:T24f8,**Describe the bug**

`POST /api/crypto/payments` tells the caller that `10.005` is too large. The service picks the amount error code by searching the validator's message, and since #32915 the validator has a third rejection that the search does not know about.

- `validatePaymentAmount` (`packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/config/crypto.ts:232-254`) rejects in three places. `:236-238` rejects an amount that is not finite or has more than two decimal places, with "Amount must be a finite USD amount in whole cents". This check came in with #32915 (`1313e200a8`). `:239-244` rejects an amount below the $5 minimum, and `:246-251` one above the $1,000 maximum. The result is `{ valid: boolean; error?: string }`, with no machine-readable reason.
- `CryptoPaymentsService.createPayment` (`packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/crypto-payments.ts:316-320`) sets `AMOUNT_TOO_SMALL` when the message contains "at least" and `AMOUNT_TOO_LARGE` otherwise. The whole-cents message has no "at least", so `10.005`, `NaN` and `Infinity` all become `AMOUNT_TOO_LARGE`. The code union (`:31-43`) has no code for an invalid amount.
- The route's schema (`packages/cloud/api/crypto/payments/route.ts:29-34`) accepts any number of decimal places (`/^(?:\d+|\d+\.\d+)$/` or a finite number between 1 and 10,000). So `10.005` reaches the service, and the route maps `AMOUNT_TOO_LARGE` to 400 `{"error":"Amount too large"}` (`:95`). `NaN` and `Infinity` cannot arrive in JSON; they reach the service only from direct callers.
- Consumers: the route is the only code that maps `CryptoPaymentError` codes (`:88-106`; any unmapped code falls through to a 500 at `:102-105`). The billing UI (`packages/ui/src/cloud/billing/components/billing-tab.tsx:429`) and the `plugin-elizacloud` billing proxy (`plugins/plugin-elizacloud/src/routes/cloud-billing-routes.ts:730-742`) show or pass through the `error` text without switching on codes. The other validator caller, `direct-wallet-payments.ts:1468-1469`, throws the message as a plain `Error`, and its route returns it as a 400 (`packages/cloud/api/crypto/direct-payments/route.ts:80-84`), so that path does not misclassify.

The repository error policy asks for typed errors and warns against branching on prose. This classification is an example: adding the third rejection message changed the error code without anyone touching the classifier.

**To Reproduce**

I mounted the real `packages/cloud/api/crypto/payments/route.ts` and called the real `cryptoPaymentsService.createPayment` from `packages/cloud/api` on `origin/develop` `4573712ebf`. Only session auth and the rate limiter were stubbed. `OXAPAY_MERCHANT_API_KEY` was set to a local placeholder so that `isOxaPayConfigured()` passes. `oxaPayService.createInvoice` was spied to throw, and it was never called.

<details><summary>Probe (scratch file <code>packages/cloud/api/__tests__/zz-amount-probe.test.ts</code>, not committed)</summary>

```ts
process.env.DATABASE_URL = "pglite://memory";
process.env.TEST_DATABASE_URL = "pglite://memory";

}));

  for (const amount of ["10.005", Number.NaN, Number.POSITIVE_INFINITY, "4.99", "1000.01"]) {

      (error: { code?: string; message?: string }) => ({ code: error.code, message: error.message }),
    );
    console.log("SERVICE", JSON.stringify({ amount: String(amount), ...outcome }));
  }
  console.log("OXAPAY_INVOICE_CALLS", invoice.mock.calls.length);
  expect(invoice).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
});
```

A $10.005 request, which is below the $1,000 maximum, is answered "Amount too large". The error's own message says the actual problem is whole cents.

**Expected behavior**

- The validator returns a typed reason alongside its message, and the service maps that reason to a code without reading the message.
- An amount that is not a finite whole-cent value gets its own code, for example `AMOUNT_INVALID`. The route answers 400 with a message that says what is wrong.
- Below-minimum and above-maximum amounts keep `AMOUNT_TOO_SMALL` and `AMOUNT_TOO_LARGE`, with their current messages and 400 responses.

**Screenshots / recording of the wrong behavior (required for anything visible)**

N/A - API error classification with no rendered surface of its own. The UI only shows the route's `error` text in a toast. The route and service transcript above is the proof.

**Evidence / reproduction proof**

- [x] MP4 recording or JPG screenshots of the broken behavior: N/A - no visible surface of its own; see the route and service transcript.
- [x] Backend logs and frontend console/network logs when relevant: route log above; N/A for frontend (the UI does not branch on the code).
- [x] Real-LLM trajectory when the bug involves agent/action/prompt/model behavior: N/A - no model, prompt or action is involved.
- [x] Domain artifacts when relevant: the HTTP status and body for each amount, and the `CryptoPaymentError` code and message from the real service, both in the transcript above.

**Additional context**

- The route's schema bounds (`route.ts:33-34`, $1 to $10,000) are looser than the service's limits ($5 to $1,000, `ORGANIZATION_CREDIT_CHECKOUT_LIMITS`). The service enforces the real bounds, so this report does not change them.
- Because JSON cannot carry `NaN` or `Infinity`, the HTTP-reachable case is an amount with more than two decimal places. The same misclassification applies to any direct caller of `createPayment`.

17:T2634,**Describe the bug**

Two replay defects remain in the durable connector hold after #32909 (head `bc812ab321`). Both are in `packages/cloud/services/gateway-webhook`, and both only affect Blooio and Twilio turns that the gateway has already acknowledged to the provider.

1. **A non-retryable Cloud refusal is retried for the whole hold budget.** `redeliverHeldWebhook` re-holds every `PersonalSharedPreEgressError` (`src/webhook-handler.ts:949-956`). That includes failures that Cloud itself classified as `retryable: false`: a 4xx, or `X-Eliza-Retryable: false`, as read by `readPersonalSharedFailureMetadata` (`packages/cloud/services/_common/src/personal-shared-failure.ts:100-102`, `:127-132`). The refused turn is sent to Cloud again on every drain for `CUTOVER_HOLD_MAX_MS` (one hour). It is then logged as `Held personal connector turn exceeded the cutover budget`, with the stale `holdCode: personal_cutover_in_progress` and the operator action "inspect the Dedicated target's attestation". A first-delivery failure of the same kind is settled at once by `settleBackgroundDeliveryFailure`.
2. **A delivered hold is replayed after its lease lapses.** `drainCutoverHolds` removes a settled record only while the lease still holds this drainer's token (`src/cutover-hold.ts:250`). Suppose renewal fails for a whole lease period and no other replica takes the lease. The turn is delivered and the ledger says `delivered`, but the record and its index entry stay. The next drain runs the Cloud turn and the provider send a second time.

**To Reproduce**

I ran the real handler and drainer (`handleWebhook`, `drainCutoverHolds`, `redeliverHeldWebhook`, `releaseExpiredHeldWebhook`) against a local `Bun.serve` stub of the Cloud personal-shared route and the in-memory Redis adapter (`MOCK_REDIS=1`, ioredis-mock). Only the Blooio provider API is substituted. The probe drives the same setup that `__tests__/cutover-hold.test.ts` uses. A signed Blooio webhook arrives while Cloud answers `503 personal_cutover_in_progress`, so the turn is held and the ledger is `held`. Then:

- P2b: Cloud is attested. While the attested turn runs, the lease key is deleted (it expired with no new owner). The probe runs one drain, then a second drain.
- P3: Cloud starts answering `400 {"code":"invalid_request"}` (`retryable: false`). The probe runs one drain at `now + 60s`, then a drain after the hold budget (`now + CUTOVER_HOLD_MAX_MS + 120s`).

<details><summary>Probe P2b and P3 (added to <code>__tests__/</code> of a checkout at <code>bc812ab321</code>)</summary>

`startCloud`, `holdOne`, `drainHandlers` and `providerSends` are the same helpers as in `__tests__/cutover-hold.test.ts`. `holdOne` sends one signed Blooio webhook and waits for the `held` ledger. The Cloud stub's `permanentStatus` returns that status with `{"success":false,"code":"invalid_request"}`.

```ts
test("P2b a delivered outcome whose lease simply expired (no replacement) is replayed", async () => {
  const cloud = startCloud();
  const redis = await holdOne(cloud, "msg_probe_p2b");
  const dedupKey = "webhook:blooio:msg_probe_p2b";
  const leaseKey = `webhook:cutover-hold-lease:${dedupKey}`;
  const heldTurns = cloud.turns.length;
  cloud.state.attested = true;
  cloud.state.onAttestedTurn = async () => {
    await redis.del(leaseKey);
  };
  const first = await drainCutoverHolds(redis, drainHandlers(cloud.origin, redis), { now: Date.now() + 60_000 });
  const ledgerAfterFirst = await redis.get(dedupKey);
  cloud.state.onAttestedTurn = undefined;
  const second = await drainCutoverHolds(redis, drainHandlers(cloud.origin, redis), { now: Date.now() + 120_000 });
  console.log("P2b", JSON.stringify({ first, ledgerAfterFirst, second, turns: cloud.turns.length - heldTurns, sends: providerSends.length }));
}, 60_000);

test("P3 a permanent non-hold Cloud 400 is retried until the hour budget, then released", async () => {
  const cloud = startCloud();
  const redis = await holdOne(cloud, "msg_probe_p3");
  const dedupKey = "webhook:blooio:msg_probe_p3";
  cloud.state.permanentStatus = 400;
  const retry = await drainCutoverHolds(redis, drainHandlers(cloud.origin, redis), { now: Date.now() + 60_000 });
  const ledgerAfterRetry = await redis.get(dedupKey);
  const expired = await drainCutoverHolds(redis, drainHandlers(cloud.origin, redis), { now: Date.now() + CUTOVER_HOLD_MAX_MS + 120_000 });
  const ledgerAfterExpiry = await redis.get(dedupKey);
  console.log("P3", JSON.stringify({ retry, ledgerAfterRetry, expired, ledgerAfterExpiry, sends: providerSends.length }));
}, 60_000);
```

</details>

```
{"level":"info","message":"Held personal connector turn delivered after cutover","platform":"blooio","messageId":"msg_probe_p2b","heldForMs":7,"attempts":1}
{"level":"info","message":"Held personal connector turn delivered after cutover","platform":"blooio","messageId":"msg_probe_p2b","heldForMs":11,"attempts":1}
{"level":"warn","message":"Personal Shared Cloud attempt failed","platform":"blooio","messageId":"msg_probe_p3","attempt":1,"status":400,"retryable":false}
{"level":"error","message":"Held personal connector turn exceeded the cutover budget","platform":"blooio","messageId":"msg_probe_p3","holdCode":"personal_cutover_in_progress","operatorAction":"inspect the Dedicated target's attestation; the turn was never executed"}
{"level":"error","message":"Background message processing failed","error":"cutover hold expired","platform":"blooio","messageId":"msg_probe_p3"}
```

</details>

P2b reports the same turn as delivered twice, with two Cloud turns and two provider sends. The first drain reports `delivered: 0`, although the ledger already reads `delivered`. P3 reschedules the refused turn instead of releasing it, and settles it only through the expiry path.

**Expected behavior**

- A redelivery that Cloud refuses with a non-retryable classification is settled at once, the same way the first background delivery settles it. `settleBackgroundDeliveryFailure` logs the failure at error level. Because no provider egress happened, it also reopens the dedup key. The drain reports `released: 1`, and later drains do nothing. Cutover holds and failures without a non-retryable classification (transport errors, 5xx) stay held, as #32909 intends.
- A redelivery whose outcome is settled removes its record unless another replica now owns the lease. A lease that simply lapsed does not keep the record. P2b should give one Cloud turn and one provider send, with the first drain reporting `delivered: 1`.

**Screenshots / recording of the wrong behavior (required for anything visible)**

N/A - server-side gateway queue behaviour with no rendered surface; the probe transcript and gateway logs above are the proof.

**Evidence / reproduction proof**

- [x] MP4 recording or JPG screenshots of the broken behavior: N/A - no visible surface; see the probe transcript.
- [x] Backend logs and frontend console/network logs when relevant: gateway logs above; N/A for frontend (no UI).
- [x] Real-LLM trajectory when the bug involves agent/action/prompt/model behavior: N/A - no model call; the Cloud route is a local stub.
- [x] Domain artifacts when relevant: the Redis ledger values and drain statistics in the probe transcript.

**Additional context**

- The #32909 review notes that the Telegram private no-reply failure (`src/webhook-handler.ts:1703-1705`, `retryable: false`) reaches this path. It does not in practice. Telegram webhooks return inside the Telegram branch (`src/webhook-handler.ts:630-713`) before the hold code (`:773-778`), so only Blooio and Twilio turns are ever held. The reachable cases are Blooio and Twilio turns that Cloud refuses with a 4xx or `X-Eliza-Retryable: false` after a hold.
- A related case is not covered here. A different replica takes the lease mid-turn and then dies without draining (probe P2 in the #32909 review). The first drainer then correctly declines to remove the record, and it is replayed once that replica's lease lapses. Guarding against it would mean checking the dedup ledger before redelivery. It needs a lease stolen by a live owner, which lease renewal is designed to prevent.

18:T5f0,Paired phones could lose a submitted turn when the stream disconnected, reuse an obsolete same-host pairing attempt, or clear a valid credential when Android replayed the connection deep link.

This change preserves accepted turns across transport disconnects, maintains paired-session authority through completion, persists terminal generation outcomes for reconnect, and binds pairing retries to the server and entered code. Credential persistence can retry without consuming the one-time code again; same-target deep-link replay preserves the paired session. Unpaired disconnected requests retain their cancellation behavior.

Full `bun run verify` passed 284/284 tasks and all final audits. 19:T740,Saved prompt automations could disappear from the feed because their creator label was compared with the owner identity. New triggers now store the trusted local owner; listing and controls use that binding rather than client-supplied provenance. Ownerless legacy records remain available only through the canonical local-owner boundary.

Presented USER credentials cannot become OWNER through loopback trust, and distinct registered OWNER principals are refused by the local-only Node surface. Direct plugin dispatch preserves the trusted access context. Runtime-owned heartbeat rows remain visible and read-only; ordinary prompt editing remains available. Editing a saved prompt now preserves its activation and wake policy; creation keeps the existing enabled/inject-now defaults. Previously a title-only edit could silently reactivate a paused prompt.

1a:T86b,Cancelling an approval- or signal-gated workflow could leave it waiting after its worker exited. Restarting a waiting run also replaced displayed event history and reused sequence numbers. In addition, successful HTTP202 control responses entered the client’s agent-resume loop, replaying the POST and eventually showing a misleading startup warning.

This uses Smithers’ durable subtree cancellation, preserves retained parent events and continues their sequence numbers, and consumes Cancel/Approval/Signal execution receipts without replay. Received terminal output, errors and continuation IDs survive a late abort. Cancelled runs now retain a neutral `cancelled` summary status; failures remain `error`. The existing Run behavior stays unchanged.

Validation: the focused branch on develop passes 135 workflow tests across 17 isolated files, 38 UI/client/editor tests, and full repository verification. Independent review passed for the cancellation repair and incremental client/status changes. The combined candidate passed verification and app build; live QA cancelled the original paused execution, retained history through restart, then completed a second gated run with one Run POST and one Cancel POST, no false warning, no model call, and the correct All-list label. The recorded cancellation HTTP response was 397ms; this is not a general latency guarantee.

Approval and Signal live interactions and model-bearing workflow execution were not part of that live run. If a native terminal result commits before its receipt is emitted, the operation fails explicitly with `WORKFLOW_TERMINAL_RESULT_UNAVAILABLE` and does not rerun the workflow. Native data stays intact. Previously overwritten display entries are retained in the existing archive/native store and are not silently reconstructed. External consumers with exhaustive execution-summary handling should accept the additive `cancelled` value.

Cost: cancellation uses one short control child and durable store operations, with no new model call or polling service. Sequence lookup scans retained history. The client correction prevents accepted control POSTs from being replayed.

Reuse that selection only through a process-local binding to the exact request, actor, state, original sources and projection. Current provider constraints and receipts remain intact; absent, forged or changed bindings use the existing full-context path.

The extractor can request complete original context before proposing an effect. Restoration stays within the existing two-call budget, and mixed restoration/plan output or unresolved output fails without creating a task. Explicit source selection takes precedence over automatic background-history projection.

Cost: adds local hashing and rendering, and can avoid the redundant history read on a valid managed path. Restoration can still require the second extraction call. Live token and latency savings are unmeasured; no model defaults or source history storage change.

1c:T3117,# Relates to

#32907 (retire creator payouts, owner decision #22957 / #22961) merged with the agent publish path still able to turn creator inference markup back on. That was the first blocking point of [this review](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/pull/32907#pullrequestreview-5345258500), and it is unchanged on develop `4573712ebf`:

- Migration 0500 set `monetization_enabled = false` on every agent, and `agentMonetizationService.updateSettings` refuses to set it back (`agent-monetization.ts:408`).
- `POST /api/v1/agents/:agentId/publish` still accepts `enableMonetization` and `markupPercentage` up to 1000 (`publish/route.ts:25-26`). `userCharactersRepository.publish` writes both straight to `user_characters` (`characters.ts:752-753`).
- The early `is_public` return means an owner only has to `DELETE` (unpublish) and then `POST` `{ enableMonetization: true, markupPercentage: 1000 }` to re-enable it.
- A2A (`a2a/route.ts:433-437`, `:531-538`) and MCP (`mcp/route.ts:482-485`, `:589-596`) reserve and settle `base + markup` whenever the stored flag is set. The creator step then calls `recordCreatorEarnings`, which throws `CreatorMonetizationRetiredError` for any positive amount (`agent-monetization.ts:235`). That happens inside a settlement task whose `.catch` only logs "Deferred settlement failed".

**Result:** the calling org pays up to 11× the inference cost, the surcharge accrues to nobody, and the retirement statement in migration 0500 no longer holds.

# Change

**Fence the write** (the only writer that could set the flag):

- `publish/route.ts` throws `CreatorMonetizationRetiredError("agent_inference_markup")` (410 `creator_monetization_retired`) when the request asks for the markup: `enableMonetization` set, or a positive `markupPercentage`.
  - The check reads the raw request before validation, so shapes that fail `PublishSchema` (`markupPercentage: 5000`, `"250"`, `enableMonetization: "true"`) are refused too, instead of being published with defaults.
  - Any other invalid body is a typed 400 instead of a silent publish with defaults.
- `agentMonetizationService.updateSettings` also refuses a positive `markupPercentage`, so the owner's settings can no longer store a markup that is never charged.
  - The check runs before any write and before the already-public early return.
  - The response reports `monetizationEnabled: false, markupPercentage: 0`.
- `userCharactersRepository.publish()` drops its two monetization options and always writes `monetization_enabled: false` and `inference_markup_percentage: "0"`, so no future caller can re-enable it.

**Backstop billing,** for rows re-enabled before this fence:

- A2A `chat` and MCP `chat` reserve and settle the base inference cost only, whatever the stored flag says.
- The unreachable creator-earnings branch is removed, along with the now-unused `calculateCreditMarkup` and `agentMonetizationService` imports.
- The response `cost` reports `markup: 0`.

**Advertise what is billed:**

- The A2A agent card no longer adds `markupPercentage` to skill pricing, and `getAgentInfo` reports `monetizationEnabled: false, markupPercentage: "0"`.
- The MCP card reports standard inference pricing, and `get_info` reports `monetization: false, markup: "0"`.
- Discovery lists every agent as `{ type: "credits", description: "Standard inference costs" }`, the same wording as the MCP card.
  - Develop labelled non-monetized agents "Free to use" even though their chat bills the caller's credits at the base inference cost, and it advertised "N% markup" for monetized ones.
- The public character view reports `monetizationEnabled: false`.

Owner-facing views of the stored setting (`GET /api/v1/agents/:id/monetization`, credits summary) are unchanged. `DELETE` (unpublish) already writes the flag off, and `updateSettings` refuses to turn it on or store a positive markup.

# Testing

- [x] Targets `develop` and has no conflicts with the latest `origin/develop` (`4573712ebf`).

This used Ubuntu 24.04 (WSL2), Bun 1.4.2 and `bun install --frozen-lockfile` on develop `4573712ebf`.

**New route tests** in `packages/cloud/api/__tests__/agent-inference-markup-retired.test.ts`. They drive the real publish, A2A and MCP routes and the real `failureResponse` / `CreatorMonetizationRetiredError`. Auth, the character lookup, pricing, the model stream and org admission are substituted, and admission records what was reserved and settled.

- Publishing with `enableMonetization: true` answers 410 `creator_monetization_retired` and writes nothing. It does the same for an already-public agent, which develop answered 200.
- Publishing with only `markupPercentage: 250` also answers the typed 410 and writes nothing. So do `markupPercentage: 5000`, `markupPercentage: "250"` and `enableMonetization: "true"`, which fail schema validation.
- Any other invalid body (`a2aEnabled: "yes"`) answers 400 and writes nothing.
- An ordinary publish passes no monetization option to the repository and reports markup off.
- A2A `chat` and MCP `chat` on a row that still stores `monetization_enabled = true, inference_markup_percentage = 1000`:
  - They reserve `{ baseTotalCost: 0.01, platformMarkup: 0, totalCost: 0.01 }` and settle the base cost `0.02`.
  - **On develop they reserve `platformMarkup: 0.1, totalCost: 0.11`.**
- The A2A card, MCP card, `getAgentInfo` and `get_info` advertise no markup for that row. On develop the A2A card lists `markupPercentage: 1000` on both skills.
- `GET /api/v1/discovery?types=agent` lists that row as `credits` / "Standard inference costs". On develop it reads "1000.00% markup on inference costs".

**Service tests** in `creator-monetization-retirement.pglite.test.ts` (real PGlite, same file as the 0500 tests):

- `publish()` on an unpublished row that stores a 250% markup leaves it public with the markup off.
- `updateSettings` with `markupPercentage: 250` throws `CreatorMonetizationRetiredError` and stores nothing. A zero markup is still accepted.

## Known gaps / failures

- `bun run verify` was not run in full on this host.
- Rows already re-enabled through the old publish path keep `monetization_enabled = true` in storage. After this change nothing bills, advertises or accrues from that flag, and the owner's own settings view still shows it. The one-off cleanup would be migration 0500's `UPDATE user_characters SET monetization_enabled = false`. It is left out to avoid racing the migration numbering.

## Deployment

None. No migration or configuration change.

1d:T5e5,## Summary
Resolves #30590. Fresh PR ported to the current package layout (`packages/core/src/utils/assistant-text.ts`), superseding #30620 following package restructure triage feedback from @lalalune.

When `stripAssistantStageDirections` strips roleplay stage directions (such as `*smiles*` or `*sighs*`), the subsequent spacing normalization previously collapsed leading whitespace with `\n[ \t]+/g`, destroying code formatting and indentation inside fenced code blocks.

### Changes
1. **CommonMark-compliant Fenced Code Block Scanner** (`packages/core/src/utils/assistant-text.ts`):
   - Implements delimiter matching for both backtick and tilde fences (>= 3).
   - Normalizes stage directions and spacing exclusively in prose segments while preserving fenced code blocks byte-for-byte without placeholders or sentinel tokens.
   - Respects container-relative closing fence indentation (CommonMark ?4.5, Example 137).
   - Preserves indentation in streaming / unterminated code blocks.
2. **Deduplication in Agent Host** (`packages/agent/src/api/chat-text-helpers.ts`):
   - Re-exports `stripAssistantStageDirections` directly from `@elizaos/core`, eliminating duplicated logic in the agent host.
3. **Comprehensive Unit Test Suite** (`packages/core/src/utils/assistant-text.test.ts`):
   - Adds unit tests verifying code indentation preservation, list-nested code blocks, comments with stage direction words inside code blocks, blockquotes, streaming code blocks, and Example 137 false closer scenarios.1e:T1014b,Bumps the dev-dependencies group with 8 updates:

Updates `jsdom` from 29.1.1 to 30.1.1
<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/jsdom/jsdom/releases">jsdom's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>v30.1.1</h2>
<ul>
<li>Fixed spurious window <code>blur</code> and <code>focusout</code> events and incorrect <code>event.relatedTarget</code> values when focusing an element after removing the previously focused element, which regressed in v30.1.0. (<a href="https://github.com/asamuzaK"><code>@​asamuzaK</code></a>)</li>
<li>Fixed focus and blur behavior across frames, and focusing the document's viewport through <code>document.documentElement.focus()</code>. (<a href="https://github.com/asamuzaK"><code>@​asamuzaK</code></a>)</li>
<li>Fixed focus targets removed or disabled by <code>blur</code> listeners becoming active, and text selections made by focus and blur listeners being overwritten. (<a href="https://github.com/asamuzaK"><code>@​asamuzaK</code></a>)</li>
<li>Fixed <code>element.focus()</code> incorrectly focusing disabled form controls and <code>&lt;input type=&quot;hidden&quot;&gt;</code> elements with <code>tabindex=&quot;&quot;</code>. (<a href="https://github.com/scttcper"><code>@​scttcper</code></a>)</li>
<li>Fixed invalid <code>style.setProperty()</code> calls changing existing <code>!important</code> priorities, serialized styles, or mutation records. (<a href="https://github.com/FedgeNo"><code>@​FedgeNo</code></a>)</li>
<li>Fixed <code>!important</code> handling when updating CSS longhands after shorthands, using variables or CSS-wide keywords, and assigning style properties directly. (<a href="https://github.com/FedgeNo"><code>@​FedgeNo</code></a>)</li>
<li>Fixed <code>&lt;noscript&gt;</code> parsing with <code>includeNodeLocations: true</code> or inside frames to honor the <code>runScripts</code> option.</li>
<li>Fixed the <code>storageQuota</code> option being ignored by frames.</li>
<li>Fixed encoding detection of HTML and XML byte input to honor XML encoding declarations and detect UTF-16 without a byte order mark.</li>
<li>Fixed exceptions caused by truncated <code>charset</code> parameters in <code>&lt;meta&gt;</code> elements, and encoding detection incorrectly using incomplete <code>&lt;meta&gt;</code> tags. (<a href="https://github.com/FedgeNo"><code>@​FedgeNo</code></a>)</li>
<li>Fixed XML serialization errors for namespaces named <code>constructor</code>, <code>toString</code>, <code>__proto__</code>, or <code>&quot;null&quot;</code>, and incorrect reuse of namespace prefixes declared on sibling elements.</li>
<li>Fixed <code>element.innerHTML</code> and <code>element.outerHTML</code> in XML documents to reject invalid characters in attribute values and avoid stack overflows on large strings.</li>
<li>Fixed selector matching for <code>:lang()</code>, <code>:nth-child(... of ...)</code> after mutations, and <code>:has()</code> with duplicate IDs or nested logical pseudo-classes. (<a href="https://github.com/asamuzaK"><code>@​asamuzaK</code></a>)</li>
</ul>
<h2>v30.1.0</h2>
<p>jsdom is feeling the AGI!</p>
<p>This release is dedicated to <a href="https://github.com/scttcper"><code>@​scttcper</code></a>, who unleashed <a href="https://github.com/codex"><code>@​codex</code></a> upon jsdom and found tons of performance improvements. Along the way, he found and fixed many correctness issues as well.</p>
<p>We really appreciate his thoughtful PRs, which did a great job following the project's contribution guidelines, and were clearly human-curated, with their PR descriptions edited to be brief and respectful of the maintainers' time.</p>
<p>Thanks to <a href="https://github.com/scttcper"><code>@​scttcper</code></a>, as well as all the other contributors of this release (most of whom were AI-assisted).</p>
<ul>
<li>Added named access to elements on <code>document</code>, such as <code>document.myForm</code> for <code>&lt;form name=&quot;myForm&quot;&gt;</code>. (<a href="https://github.com/vojtisprime11"><code>@​vojtisprime11</code></a>)</li>
<li>Added <code>QuotaExceededError</code>, including its use for storage quota errors and oversized <code>crypto.getRandomValues()</code> requests.</li>
<li>Added support for the relaxed DOM naming rules when creating elements, attributes, and document types.</li>
<li>Improved performance of DOM construction, tree mutations, range operations, and live collection access, especially on large documents. (<a href="https://github.com/scttcper"><code>@​scttcper</code></a>, <a href="https://github.com/erezrokah"><code>@​erezrokah</code></a>)</li>
<li>Improved performance of <code>getComputedStyle()</code>, style changes, and CSS serialization. (<a href="https://github.com/scttcper"><code>@​scttcper</code></a>, <a href="https://github.com/jhult"><code>@​jhult</code></a>)</li>
<li>Improved performance of event dispatch, form control and label lookups, and updates to <code>&lt;select&gt;</code> elements and radio button groups. (<a href="https://github.com/scttcper"><code>@​scttcper</code></a>)</li>
<li>Reduced memory use when creating and working with DOM nodes, attributes, event listeners, and mutation observers. (<a href="https://github.com/scttcper"><code>@​scttcper</code></a>)</li>
<li>Changed <code>window.close()</code> to preserve access to the document and its DOM through retained references.</li>
<li>Fixed <code>element.querySelectorAll()</code> returning no matches when the first part of the selector matches the element itself, which regressed in v30.0.0. (<a href="https://github.com/asamuzaK"><code>@​asamuzaK</code></a>)</li>
<li>Fixed case sensitivity in CSS attribute selectors, including selectors matching <code>data-state=&quot;&quot;</code>, <code>title=&quot;&quot;</code>, and other case-sensitive values. (<a href="https://github.com/asamuzaK"><code>@​asamuzaK</code></a>)</li>
<li>Fixed <code>document.querySelector()</code> failing to find a matching element when an earlier element has the same ID but does not match the rest of the selector. (<a href="https://github.com/vojtisprime11"><code>@​vojtisprime11</code></a>)</li>
<li>Fixed <code>:focus</code> matching in shadow trees. (<a href="https://github.com/asamuzaK"><code>@​asamuzaK</code></a>)</li>
<li>Fixed DOM insertion and replacement, including valid <code>document.replaceChildren()</code> calls, invalid document element and doctype placements, and mutations during <code>element.replaceWith()</code>.</li>
<li>Fixed the ordering of script execution, custom element callbacks, iframe loading, and mutation observer notifications during DOM insertion, including in shadow trees.</li>
<li>Fixed queued events and navigation continuing after <code>window.close()</code> or iframe removal, and prevented new scripts, resource loads, timers, and animation frames from starting in destroyed documents. (<a href="https://github.com/scttcper"><code>@​scttcper</code></a>)</li>
<li>Fixed parent documents waiting indefinitely for loading to finish when a child iframe removes itself during loading.</li>
<li>Fixed request cancellation across redirects, during pending <code>requestInterceptor()</code> callbacks, and when reusing an <code>XMLHttpRequest</code> after aborting it.</li>
<li>Fixed resource loading and <code>JSDOM.fromURL()</code> potentially hanging when response handling throws and response stream cleanup does not finish.</li>
<li>Fixed successful cached resource loads being treated as aborted.</li>
<li>Fixed <code>getComputedStyle()</code> and <code>document.styleSheets</code> using the wrong stylesheet order after inserting or updating <code>&lt;style&gt;</code> elements.</li>
<li>Fixed <code>getComputedStyle()</code> ignoring nested <code>@import</code> and <code>@media</code> rules in imported stylesheets, and returning stale results after imports finish loading.</li>
<li>Fixed style invalidation, stylesheet removal, and frame source updates in shadow trees.</li>
<li>Fixed repeated <code>getComputedStyle()</code> calls changing case-sensitive background URLs, and inconsistent resolution of border shorthands containing system colors. (<a href="https://github.com/scttcper"><code>@​scttcper</code></a>)</li>
<li>Fixed computed border widths, including borderless elements incorrectly reporting <code>16px</code>, which regressed in v30.0.0. (<a href="https://github.com/Alberto-BaseNet"><code>@​Alberto-BaseNet</code></a>)</li>
<li>Fixed <code>getComputedStyle()</code> to resolve <code>'font-weight'</code> keywords to numeric values. (<a href="https://github.com/tianrking"><code>@​tianrking</code></a>)</li>
<li>Fixed <code>getComputedStyle()</code> to convert lengths to pixels inside CSS math functions containing percentages, and to resolve percentages in <code>'font-size'</code> math functions. (<a href="https://github.com/soroushm"><code>@​soroushm</code></a>)</li>
</ul>
<!-- raw HTML omitted -->
</blockquote>

Updates `@typescript-eslint/parser` from 8.70.0 to 8.70.1
<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/typescript-eslint/typescript-eslint/releases">@​typescript-eslint/parser's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>v8.70.1</h2>
<h2>8.70.1 (2026-09-21)</h2>
<h3>🩹 Fixes</h3>
<ul>
<li><strong>ast-spec:</strong> narrow import attribute keys to identifiers and strings (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/typescript-eslint/typescript-eslint/pull/12879">#12879</a>)</li>
<li><strong>eslint-plugin:</strong> [no-useless-default-assignment] avoid false positives on tuples with a rest element (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/typescript-eslint/typescript-eslint/pull/12768">#12768</a>)</li>
<li><strong>eslint-plugin:</strong> [no-unnecessary-type-parameters] handle type precedence in the suggestion fixer (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/typescript-eslint/typescript-eslint/pull/12637">#12637</a>)</li>
<li><strong>eslint-plugin:</strong> [no-explicit-any] use unknown[] for bare any rest parameters (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/typescript-eslint/typescript-eslint/pull/12818">#12818</a>)</li>
<li><strong>eslint-plugin:</strong> [no-generated-empty-object-type] don't report a mapped type whose keys are not resolved yet (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/typescript-eslint/typescript-eslint/pull/12854">#12854</a>)</li>
<li><strong>eslint-plugin:</strong> [no-misused-spread] omit WeakMap spread suggestions (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/typescript-eslint/typescript-eslint/pull/12850">#12850</a>)</li>
<li><strong>eslint-plugin:</strong> [no-unnecessary-type-assertion] false positive for empty object asserted to a type alias of Record (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/typescript-eslint/typescript-eslint/pull/12869">#12869</a>)</li>
<li><strong>eslint-plugin:</strong> [no-meaningless-void-operator] allow void on assignment expressions (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/typescript-eslint/typescript-eslint/pull/12873">#12873</a>)</li>
<li><strong>eslint-plugin:</strong> [await-thenable] prevent autofix from breaking code when removing <code>await</code> (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/typescript-eslint/typescript-eslint/pull/12716">#12716</a>)</li>
<li><strong>eslint-plugin:</strong> [no-unnecessary-parameter-property-assignment] account for parameter reassignment (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/typescript-eslint/typescript-eslint/pull/12880">#12880</a>)</li>
<li><strong>eslint-plugin:</strong> [unbound-method] treat Intl.Collator.prototype.compare as spec-bound (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/typescript-eslint/typescript-eslint/pull/12845">#12845</a>)</li>
<li><strong>eslint-plugin:</strong> [no-unnecessary-condition] handle union-keyed index access on the left-hand side of nullish assignment (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/typescript-eslint/typescript-eslint/pull/12747">#12747</a>)</li>
<li><strong>eslint-plugin:</strong> [no-useless-default-assignment] convert the fixer to a suggestion fixer (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/typescript-eslint/typescript-eslint/pull/12826">#12826</a>)</li>
<li><strong>eslint-plugin:</strong> [no-misused-promises] handle multiple Promise constituents (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/typescript-eslint/typescript-eslint/pull/12904">#12904</a>)</li>
<li><strong>rule-tester:</strong> test the final autofix output instead of the first pass (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/typescript-eslint/typescript-eslint/pull/12867">#12867</a>)</li>
<li><strong>scope-manager:</strong> merge implicit global definitions (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/typescript-eslint/typescript-eslint/pull/12809">#12809</a>)</li>
<li><strong>type-utils:</strong> match package specifiers on whole path components (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/typescript-eslint/typescript-eslint/pull/12838">#12838</a>)</li>
<li><strong>typescript-estree:</strong> resolve symlinked paths when matching files to projects (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/typescript-eslint/typescript-eslint/pull/12725">#12725</a>)</li>
<li><strong>typescript-estree:</strong> add missing <code>&lt;</code> token opening type arguments (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/typescript-eslint/typescript-eslint/pull/12821">#12821</a>)</li>
<li><strong>typescript-estree:</strong> require string literal import attribute values (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/typescript-eslint/typescript-eslint/pull/12894">#12894</a>)</li>
<li><strong>website:</strong> prevent playground from breaking down after opening link with the .js file type (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/typescript-eslint/typescript-eslint/pull/12777">#12777</a>)</li>
</ul>

Updates `postal-mime` from 3.0.0 to 4.0.0
<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/postalsys/postal-mime/releases">postal-mime's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>v4.0.0</h2>
<h2><a href="https://github.com/postalsys/postal-mime/compare/v3.0.1...v4.0.0">4.0.0</a> (2026-09-26)</h2>
<h3>⚠ BREAKING CHANGES</h3>
<ul>
<li>Node.js 20 or newer is required.</li>
<li><code>Attachment.disposition</code> is typed as <code>AttachmentDisposition | null</code>, which accepts any Content-Disposition token, instead of <code>'attachment' | 'inline' | null</code>. The parsed output is unchanged, but code that assigns the value to the narrower type has to widen it.</li>
<li>the ES module entry point moved from src/postal-mime.js to dist/esm/postal-mime.js, so browser code that imported the file from node_modules directly has to use the new path. Consumers that import <code>postal-mime</code> by name are unaffected.</li>
</ul>
<h3>Features</h3>
<ul>
<li>convert to TypeScript and ship ES module and CommonJS builds (<a href="https://github.com/postalsys/postal-mime/commit/2559c820af2553d9166af586868ec636ea69e820">2559c82</a>)</li>
<li>declare the CommonJS entry point with export = and ship source maps (<a href="https://github.com/postalsys/postal-mime/commit/84d11596d8fcf667de7805c1e46a98d7734786b1">84d1159</a>)</li>
<li>type the attachment disposition as the token the message carries (<a href="https://github.com/postalsys/postal-mime/commit/4bb27965cb1c39981d1571c082b353085288df6c">4bb2796</a>)</li>
</ul>
<h3>Bug Fixes</h3>
<ul>
<li>keep the public object types assignable to an index signature (<a href="https://github.com/postalsys/postal-mime/commit/336303952f3862f4397f58abf7f586de659f93d3">3363039</a>)</li>
</ul>
<h3>Miscellaneous Chores</h3>
<ul>
<li>require Node.js 20 or newer (<a href="https://github.com/postalsys/postal-mime/commit/21eaae6553102e955c7de01f74a0deb7022d5990">21eaae6</a>)</li>
</ul>
<h2>v3.0.1</h2>
<h2><a href="https://github.com/postalsys/postal-mime/compare/v3.0.0...v3.0.1">3.0.1</a> (2026-09-25)</h2>
<h3>Bug Fixes</h3>
<ul>
<li>collect parsed addresses in linear time (<a href="https://github.com/postalsys/postal-mime/commit/68cd065f92c2cb64227b91cab7ba1409d89b9824">68cd065</a>), closes <a href="https://redirect.github.com/postalsys/postal-mime/issues/97">#97</a></li>
<li>convert html to text in linear time (<a href="https://github.com/postalsys/postal-mime/commit/38b2a839ca1f83bf570f7628375c1330f4495aef">38b2a83</a>), closes <a href="https://redirect.github.com/postalsys/postal-mime/issues/97">#97</a></li>
<li>detect angle bracket addresses in decoded words in linear time (<a href="https://github.com/postalsys/postal-mime/commit/390be2b4395ff46458ff7b8bf3d148d1e0997e0e">390be2b</a>), closes <a href="https://redirect.github.com/postalsys/postal-mime/issues/97">#97</a></li>
<li>normalize calendar attachment line endings in linear time (<a href="https://github.com/postalsys/postal-mime/commit/36edda70629074622a6b8d1529b2366ff2cfa71d">36edda7</a>), closes <a href="https://redirect.github.com/postalsys/postal-mime/issues/97">#97</a></li>
<li>strip header comments in linear time (<a href="https://github.com/postalsys/postal-mime/commit/5d877da9b700059f14318251083d62c9dd116c5a">5d877da</a>), closes <a href="https://redirect.github.com/postalsys/postal-mime/issues/97">#97</a></li>
<li>trim header whitespace in linear time (<a href="https://github.com/postalsys/postal-mime/commit/932441fc984b0c86804d55e504d5cd11c290b763">932441f</a>), closes <a href="https://redirect.github.com/postalsys/postal-mime/issues/97">#97</a></li>
<li>trim text around an extracted address in linear time (<a href="https://github.com/postalsys/postal-mime/commit/511a19482dca01c1225d2bc01ea33409ba9233ad">511a194</a>), closes <a href="https://redirect.github.com/postalsys/postal-mime/issues/97">#97</a></li>
<li>unfold format=flowed text in linear time (<a href="https://github.com/postalsys/postal-mime/commit/237c7a98debf1bf9308e40b54a6d6662aba5cdf6">237c7a9</a>), closes <a href="https://redirect.github.com/postalsys/postal-mime/issues/97">#97</a></li>
</ul>
<h3>Performance Improvements</h3>
<ul>
<li>skip decoder construction for charset labels TextDecoder cannot know (<a href="https://github.com/postalsys/postal-mime/commit/5407771692d0b989e9109a9edb3d1d33df62cd64">5407771</a>), closes <a href="https://redirect.github.com/postalsys/postal-mime/issues/97">#97</a></li>
</ul>
</blockquote>

Updates `rollup` from 4.63.4 to 4.63.5
<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/rollup/rollup/releases">rollup's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>v4.63.5</h2>
<h2>4.63.5</h2>
<p><em>2026-09-24</em></p>
<h3>Bug Fixes</h3>
<ul>
<li>Fix an issue where watch mode would hang instead of terminating when closing via Ctrl+C (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rollup/rollup/issues/6521">#6521</a>)</li>
<li>Avoid starting overlapping watch mode runs when plugins invalidate files at the wrong time (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rollup/rollup/issues/6526">#6526</a>)</li>
<li>Fix many edge cases where watch mode events were not properly emitted to listeners, especially when errors occur (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rollup/rollup/issues/6526">#6526</a>)</li>
</ul>
<h3>Pull Requests</h3>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://redirect.github.com/rollup/rollup/pull/6520">#6520</a>: Lock file maintenance (<a href="https://github.com/renovate"><code>@​renovate</code></a>[bot], <a href="https://github.com/lukastaegert"><code>@​lukastaegert</code></a>)</li>

# Change

Record removal in `drainCutoverHolds` is fenced to the lease owner (`packages/cloud/services/gateway-webhook/src/cutover-hold.ts:255-258`). When a drainer settles a turn while a *different* replica owns the lease, the record survives. If that replica dies without draining, the next drainer acquires the lapsed lease, finds a live record, and redelivers a turn whose dedup ledger already reads `delivered`. The Cloud turn and the provider send run a second time.

The drainer now reads the dedup ledger after acquiring the lease and before the expiry check (`src/cutover-hold.ts:281-282`). Only a `held` ledger is redelivered. `holdWebhookForCutover` writes `held` with no TTL, so while a record exists the ledger leaves `held` only when a drainer settles the turn: `delivered` after a completed redelivery, or reopened (absent) after a non-retryable release. Any such value means the turn is settled, so the record is removed under the same owner fence and counted in a new `stale` statistic (`CutoverHoldDrainStats.stale`, `:173`), with an info log naming the key and the ledger value. The ordering matters: the check runs before the hold-budget expiry branch, because `release(held, "expired")` would otherwise reopen a `delivered` ledger for a settled turn.

Risk: low. The only changed path is a record whose ledger is not `held` at drain time; every other outcome is unchanged. `stale` is additive on the stats object; the drain summary in `src/index.ts` now counts it in its non-empty check and logs it with the other counts, and the tests assert it.

Not addressed: two live drainers running the same turn concurrently after a renewal failure. The ledger stays `held` until a turn settles, so this check cannot see that window; lease renewal is the guard for it.

# Testing

Environment: Bun 1.3.14 on this machine (the repository pins 1.4.2), Node 24.15.0, `@elizaos/core` resolved from source through `--conditions=eliza-source` (no `dist` in this checkout). Not CI parity. All runs from `packages/cloud/services/gateway-webhook`.

New test, `a hold settled while another replica owned the lease is not redelivered once that lease lapses` (`__tests__/cutover-hold.test.ts:486`): drives the real `handleWebhook` and `drainCutoverHolds` against the local `Bun.serve` Cloud stub and in-memory Redis. While the attested turn runs, the lease key is overwritten with another replica's token; after the first drain the lease is deleted and a second drain runs. It asserts that the record survives the first drain (fenced), that the second drain reports `stale: 1` and nothing else, one Cloud turn, one provider send, the record removed, and a `delivered` ledger. The six existing `toEqual` stats assertions gain `stale: 0`.

22:T21a0,# Relates to

#32930 (expire stale sleep decisions in merged schedule state) fixed the **local** merge. The **Cloud** merge of the same device's synced rows can still report the owner asleep after they woke. These were the two blocking points of [this review](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/pull/32930#pullrequestreview-5353799309). Both are unchanged on develop `4573712ebf`.

**1. Every synced row gets the batch's send time.**

- `refreshEffectiveScheduleState` sends the device's last 48 h of `local_inference` rows in one batch, with `observedAt: now` and no per-row time (`reminders-service.ts:2306-2313`, serializer at `:1997-2023`).
- `recordsFromSyncRequest` stamps that one value on every record (`schedule-state.ts:518-532`), and the upsert keeps it (`schedule-projections.ts:678`).
- In the Cloud merge, an old sleep row therefore has the same `observedAt` as the newer wake row from the same device. It survives the per-device supersession (`schedule-state.ts:659-679`), and the sleep-first tie-break (`STATE_RANK`, `:52`) picks `sleeping`.

**2. A synced state row adopts its snapshot's meal forecast as its label.**

- `recordFromSyncInput` sets `mealLabel: args.input.mealLabel ?? snapshot.nextMealLabel ?? null` (`schedule-state.ts:508`).
- Local state rows have `mealLabel: null`, but their snapshot carries the insight's `nextMealLabel` (for example the morning's `breakfast` forecast).
- In the Cloud those rows become labelled rows. Labelled rows are excluded from the circadian vote (`:665`, `:675`), so a wake row that forecasts breakfast drops out, and the old sleep row decides.

Replaying a device's rows through the Cloud path on develop (sleep at 07:00, wake at 13:30, merged at 14:00):

| Wake row | Local merge | Cloud merge on develop |
| --- | --- | --- |
| no meal forecast | `waking` | **`sleeping`**, `currentSleepStartedAt` 07:00 |
| snapshot forecasts `breakfast` | `waking` | **`sleeping`**, `currentSleepStartedAt` 07:00 |

# Change

- **Contract:** `SyncLifeOpsScheduleObservationInput` (plugin-elizacloud) gains an optional `observedAt`, the time the device recorded that row.
- **Serializer:** it moves into `schedule-state.ts` as `scheduleObservationSyncInput` and includes the row's `observedAt`. `RemindersDomain.serializeScheduleObservationForSync` delegates to it. The tests use the real serializer.
- **Ingest:** `ingestScheduleObservations` normalizes the per-row `observedAt`. Before this change the field would have been dropped silently.
- **`recordsFromSyncRequest`:** each row keeps its own `observedAt`. A missing value, or one after the batch's send time, falls back to the send time (the previous behavior), so a device clock cannot place a row in the future.
- **`recordFromSyncInput`:** a synced row's `mealLabel` is only the label it was sent with. Real meal rows are always sent with an explicit label (`deriveLocalScheduleObservations`), and the snapshot still keeps `nextMealLabel`.

Older senders that omit the per-row time keep the current behavior. An older Cloud agent drops the unknown field during normalization.

# Testing

- [x] Targets `develop` and has no conflicts with the latest `origin/develop` (`4573712ebf`).
- [ ] Ran `bun install` and `bun run verify` after syncing; record blockers below.
- [ ] Updated relevant documentation.

This used Ubuntu 24.04 (WSL2), Bun 1.4.2 and `bun install --frozen-lockfile` on develop `4573712ebf`.

The new tests are in `schedule-state-freshness.test.ts` (vitest). Each one builds the device's stored rows, serializes them with `scheduleObservationSyncInput`, ingests them with `recordsFromSyncRequest` as `device_sync` in one batch sent at `now`, and merges with `scope: "cloud"`:

- *a synced batch keeps each row's own time, so replayed sleep does not beat the newer wake*: `waking`, `currentSleepStartedAt: null`, and the row times are preserved.
- *a synced state row does not adopt its snapshot's meal forecast as its own label*: both rows have `mealLabel: null`, and the merge result is `waking`.
- *an explicit meal row keeps its label through sync*.
- *a row time after the batch send time falls back to the send time*.

**Develop reproduction:** a throwaway test (not committed) serialized rows exactly as develop's `serializeScheduleObservationForSync` does and ran both scenarios against develop's `schedule-state.ts`:

```
REPRO-1 cloud: sleeping 2026-09-29T07:00:00.000Z
REPRO-2 cloud: sleeping 2026-09-29T07:00:00.000Z
   × develop: replayed sleep vs newer wake (no meal forecast)
   × develop: wake row whose snapshot forecasts breakfast
```

# Change

1. **The validator returns a typed reason.** `validatePaymentAmount` (`packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/config/crypto.ts:229-265`) now returns `{ valid: true }` or `{ valid: false; reason; error }`. `reason` is one of `not_whole_cents`, `below_minimum` or `above_maximum`. The checks, their order and the `error` messages are unchanged. The existing callers only read `valid` and `error`, and they compile unchanged, including `direct-wallet-payments.ts:1468-1469`. That caller throws the message as a plain `Error` and never classified it.
2. **The service maps reasons to codes through a table, not the message.** `AMOUNT_REJECTION_CODES` (`packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/crypto-payments.ts:66-70`) is checked with `satisfies Record<PaymentAmountRejection, CryptoPaymentErrorCode>`, so a new reason will not compile without a code. `not_whole_cents` maps to the new `AMOUNT_INVALID` (`:39`), `below_minimum` to `AMOUNT_TOO_SMALL`, and `above_maximum` to `AMOUNT_TOO_LARGE`. `createPayment` throws with the validator's message (`:329`). The old `|| "Invalid amount"` fallback could never be reached, because every rejection carries a message.
3. **The route maps the new code to 400.** `packages/cloud/api/crypto/payments/route.ts:96-99` adds `AMOUNT_INVALID → 400 "Amount must be a USD value in whole cents"`. Without that entry the code would fall through to the 500 default (`:106-109`).

Consumers checked with `git grep -n "AMOUNT_TOO_LARGE\|AMOUNT_TOO_SMALL\|CryptoPaymentError\|validatePaymentAmount"` across `packages/` and `plugins/`. The route above is the only code that maps these codes. The billing UI (`packages/ui/src/cloud/billing/components/billing-tab.tsx:429`) and the `plugin-elizacloud` billing proxy (`plugins/plugin-elizacloud/src/routes/cloud-billing-routes.ts:730-742`) show or forward the `error` text and do not switch on codes, so neither needed a change. The direct-wallet route (`packages/cloud/api/crypto/direct-payments/route.ts:80-84`) returns the validator message as a 400, and that message is unchanged.

Risk: low. Valid, too-small and too-large amounts get the same code, message, HTTP status and response body as before. The tests below assert the exact response bytes. The only change callers see is the response for an amount with more than two decimal places: 400 "Amount must be a USD value in whole cents" instead of 400 "Amount too large". `NaN` and `Infinity` now produce the `AMOUNT_INVALID` code, but they reach `createPayment` only from direct callers, because JSON cannot carry them.

# Change

Both defects affect only Blooio and Twilio turns that the gateway has already acknowledged to the provider and parked for a Shared→Dedicated cutover.

1. **Non-retryable Cloud refusals are released at once.** `redeliverHeldWebhook` re-held every `PersonalSharedPreEgressError`. That included failures that Cloud classified as `retryable: false`: a 4xx, or `X-Eliza-Retryable: false`, as read by `readPersonalSharedFailureMetadata` (`packages/cloud/services/_common/src/personal-shared-failure.ts:100-102`, `:127-132`). Such a turn was sent to Cloud again on every drain for the one-hour hold budget, and then logged as a cutover expiry. The guard (`src/webhook-handler.ts:954-957`) now keeps a turn held only when Cloud returned a cutover hold, or when the failure is not classified as non-retryable. A non-retryable failure falls through to `settleBackgroundDeliveryFailure` (`:963`), the same path a first background delivery takes. That path logs `Background message processing failed` at error level. Because a pre-egress failure means no provider egress happened, it also reopens the dedup key (`:835-867`). This is the ledger contract that function documents for idempotent pre-egress failures, so a refused redelivery ends in the same state as a refused first delivery. Transport errors, 5xx responses and cutover holds stay in the durable queue, as #32909 intends.
2. **A settled record is no longer kept because its lease lapsed.** `drainCutoverHolds` removed a delivered or released record only while the lease still held this drainer's token. Suppose renewal failed for a whole lease period and no other replica took the lease. The ledger already read `delivered`, but the record and index entry stayed, and the next drain ran the Cloud turn and the provider send again. The removal script (`src/cutover-hold.ts:247-260`) now refuses only when a *different* owner holds the lease. The existing tests `an expired drainer cannot mutate a replacement lease` and `a drainer releases only its own lease` still pass unchanged. The reschedule write (`:139`) keeps its strict token fence. With no lease, a `held` outcome is simply not stored, and the unchanged record is retried on the next drain.

Risk: low. Only two paths change: the redelivery of a failure with `retryable: false`, and record removal when the lease key is absent. Every other outcome is unchanged. That covers cutover holds, transient failures, a lease owned by another replica, and expiry.

Not addressed: a lease taken by another replica that dies without draining (probe P2 in the #32909 review). The first drainer still correctly declines to remove the record, and the record is replayed once that replica's lease lapses. Covering it would mean checking the dedup ledger before redelivery. It needs a live owner to steal the lease, which lease renewal is designed to prevent.

# Testing

Environment: Bun 1.3.14 on this machine (the repository pins 1.4.2), Node 24.15.0. `@elizaos/core` resolves from source through `--conditions=eliza-source` because no `dist` is built in this checkout. This is not CI parity. Prepared on the #32909 head `bc812ab321` as commit `332bb56054`, then rebased as the single commit `190d8a3304` onto `origin/develop` `4573712ebf`, which already contains #32909.

The tests are in `packages/cloud/services/gateway-webhook/__tests__/cutover-hold.test.ts`. - `a delivered hold whose lease lapsed with no new owner is not replayed`: the lease key is deleted while the attested turn runs. The test asserts 1 Cloud turn, 1 provider send, `delivered: 1` on the first drain, the record removed, all-zero stats on the next drain, and a `delivered` ledger.
- `a held turn Cloud refuses as not retryable is released at once, not replayed for the hold budget`: Cloud answers 400. The test asserts `released: 1`, a reopened (null) ledger, the record removed, all-zero stats on a drain after the hold budget, 1 Cloud turn and 0 provider sends.

25:T705,## Summary

Fixes the web dev UI crashing into the generic "Something went wrong" error boundary in `<AppProviderInner>` on any machine with the React DevTools extension installed.

## Root cause

`redactLogValue` (`packages/core/src/security/log-redaction.ts`) cloned log payloads into null-prototype (`Object.create(null)`) targets. Null-prototype objects throw `TypeError: Cannot convert object to primitive value` on `String()`/template coercion. React DevTools patches `console.info` with exactly such a coercion, so the startup logger call in `useStartupCoordinator.ts:242` (`logger.info('[startup-coordinator] phase=...')`) threw on every phase change, tripping the `ErrorBoundary`.

Repro (before fix): `String(redactTrailingArgs([{}])[0])` throws. Only machines *with* the extension were affected, which is why clean headless sessions loaded fine.

## Fix

Clone targets are now plain `{}` objects via a `createRedactClone()` helper. The `__proto__` protection never came from the prototype — it comes from `defineSafeProperty` using `Object.defineProperty`, which creates an own data property without invoking the inherited setter. Behavior of redaction is otherwise unchanged (credential masking, fail-closed markers).

## Validation

- New `packages/core/src/security/log-redaction.test.ts` (5 tests): coercion survival for plain/Map/Set clones, `__proto__` neutrality (no prototype pollution), credential masking at depth, fail-closed getter behavior — all pass.
- `tsc --noEmit -p packages/core/tsconfig.json` clean.
- `biome check` clean on both files.
- Verified end-to-end: `bun run dev` serves the UI (200) with the fix loaded.

## Files

- `packages/core/src/security/log-redaction.ts` (3 clone sites + doc comment)
- `packages/core/src/security/log-redaction.test.ts` (new)26:T1f5e,# Relates to

#32907 added funding-stop retention (#22967, owner decision #22957). A lapsed Plus/Pro plan can lose its retention clock on the first reconcile pass. That was the second blocking point of [this review](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/pull/32907#pullrequestreview-5345258500), and it is unchanged on develop `4573712ebf`.

In `agent-funding-retention.ts`:

- `discoverPlanLapses` (`:229-280`) creates a `subscription_lapsed` clock for any fallback in `fallback_pending` or `shared_active` with a `retain_until`. It never looks at the runtime.
- The fallback gets `retain_until` when it is created (`personal-dedicated-fallback.ts:340-357`).
- `activateInTransaction` (`personal-dedicated-fallback.ts:434-490`) commits `shared_active` in the same transaction that only **enqueues** the Dedicated suspend. On a lifecycle-admission conflict it leaves the fallback in `fallback_pending`. Either way the runtime is still `running` (or `provisioning`) when discovery first sees the lapse.
- `closeReason` (`:443-452`) returns `funding_restored` only when the fallback has left the withdrawn states. Otherwise it falls through to the agent-status check (`:473-478`), which returns `funding_restored` for a running agent, and for a provisioning one while the clock is `retained`.
- `reconcile` advances every open clock right after discovery, so the new clock is closed in the same pass.
- Discovery left-joins `agent_funding_retentions` on `fallback_id` (and migration 0501 has a unique index on it), so a fallback that already has a row, even a closed one, is never discovered again.

**Result:** that lapse gets no 7-day or 1-day notice and no deletion at the deadline. Its Dedicated container is retained indefinitely at no charge, which contradicts the 30-day policy.

# Change

`closeReason`: for a `subscription_lapsed` clock, the fallback interval decides.

- It returns `funding_restored` when the fallback is missing or has left `fallback_pending` / `shared_active`, as before.
- While the fallback is still withdrawn it now returns `null` (keep open) instead of falling through to the runtime status.
- `credits_exhausted` clocks are unchanged.

Deletion is unchanged too. It still requires a `stopped` runtime with no lifecycle job and no active resume (`:400-401`), so a runtime that is still shutting down only receives the notices, which is correct because the plan has lapsed.

The file header now states the two close conditions separately.

This used Ubuntu 24.04 (WSL2), Bun 1.4.2 and `bun install --frozen-lockfile` on develop `4573712ebf`.

The new cases are in `agent-funding-retention.pglite.test.ts`. They use the same PGlite database, real migrations (0480/0490/0501), real `ProvisioningJobService` and fake clock as the existing retention tests. There are two cases:
- `shared_active` fallback with the runtime `running`;
- `fallback_pending` fallback with the runtime `provisioning`.

Each case:
1. The first reconcile discovers the lapse and must leave the clock `retained`. **On develop it reports `{ discovered: 1, closed: 1 }`.**
2. The suspend lands (runtime `stopped`) while the plan is still withdrawn, and the clock stays `retained`.
3. The fallback moves to `recovery_pending`, and only then does the clock close as `funding_restored`.

## Known gaps / failures

- `bun run verify` was not run in full on this host.
- Clocks already closed as `funding_restored` by this path on a fallback that is still withdrawn stay closed. Discovery cannot recreate them because of the `fallback_id` unique index from 0501.
  - An operator query finds them: closed `subscription_lapsed` rows whose fallback is still `fallback_pending` / `shared_active`.
  - Reopening them needs an owner decision on the deadline, so it is not automated here.
- A fallback that leaves `recovery_pending` for `fallback_pending` again (withdrawn during recovery) reuses the same interval. Its earlier clock was closed at recovery and is not reopened. That behavior is unchanged and is a separate policy question.

27:T1c9c,# Relates to

#32926 (expose remote local-model readiness accurately) merged with the readiness probe gated on "`eliza-local-inference` is the sole text provider". The real plugin never satisfies that gate. This was the blocking point of [this review](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/pull/32926#pullrequestreview-5346555225); the follow-up commit reworked the availability check but kept the gate, and it is unchanged on develop `4573712ebf`.

- `readLocalTextModelReadiness` (`packages/agent/src/api/health-routes.ts:494-501`) returns `null` as soon as any text registration has a provider other than `eliza-local-inference`.
- The local-inference plugin registers its text handlers and then always installs its prefer-local router:
  - `installLocalRouterAndMark` → `installRouterHandler` (`ensure-local-inference-handler.ts:1597-1605`, called at `:1773`, `:1796` and `:1980`);
  - that registers `eliza-router` on each text slot at `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER` (`router-handler.ts:94`, `:711-721`).
- `runtime.getModelRegistrations()` includes the router, so every real host looks "mixed". The probe returns `null`, and `computeCanRespond` never sees `model_not_loaded`.
- The HTTP fixture in `local-model-readiness-http.test.ts` registers only the bare `eliza-local-inference` handler, so the tests never hit this.

**Result:** on a desktop host with no local text model assigned or loaded, `/api/status` and the WebSocket status report `canRespond: true` and `localModelReadiness: null`. The composer goes live, and the first turn is refused by the router's own pre-model hook ("No local text model is assigned or loaded.", `router-handler.ts:665-707`).

# Change

`readLocalTextModelReadiness` ignores `eliza-router` registrations.

- The router serves nothing itself; it dispatches to the other registered handlers.
- A runtime whose only real text provider is `eliza-local-inference` now reports `available` / `model_not_loaded` as #32926 intended.
- Any real remote provider behind the router (`openai`, `elizaOSCloud`, a custom provider, and so on) still makes the probe return `null`, so mixed hosts keep their current behavior.

`isCloudTextModelUnavailable` is unchanged. The router is only installed together with a local or device text handler, which is already a non-Cloud failover for that type, so ignoring the router there would not change its answer.

# Testing

- [x] Targets `develop` and has no conflicts with the latest `origin/develop` (`4573712ebf`).
- [ ] Ran `bun install` and `bun run verify` after syncing; record blockers below.
- [ ] Updated relevant documentation.

This used Ubuntu 24.04 (WSL2), Bun 1.4.2 and `bun install --frozen-lockfile` on develop `4573712ebf`.

`packages/agent/test/local-model-readiness-http.test.ts` (vitest, real `AgentRuntime` and authenticated HTTP/WebSocket server):

- The shared fixture now also registers `eliza-router` on `TEXT_SMALL` / `TEXT_LARGE` at `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER`, which is what `installRouterHandler` does. The existing HTTP load/unload test and the WebSocket status test therefore run against the real registration shape.
- New: *treats the local router as part of the local provider, not as a second provider.*
  - Router plus local handler: `model_not_loaded` and `canRespond: false`.
  - Adding an `openai` handler: readiness `null` and `canRespond: true`.

</details>29:T1c64,# Relates to

#32910 (subscriptions + pay-as-you-go pricing policy) added the Cloud-credit refund path for crypto and x402 payments (#22968). For an x402 **payment request**, that path credits the payee instead of the payer. This was the blocking point of [this review](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/pull/32910#pullrequestreview-5345370970). The later `status === "confirmed"` guard (#32915) did not change the recipient, so it is unchanged on develop `4573712ebf`.

- `x402PaymentRequestsService.create` stores the `crypto_payments` row under `organization_id: input.organizationId, user_id: input.userId` (`x402-payment-requests.ts:650-651`). The route passes the **requesting** user's organization (`api/v1/x402/requests/route.ts:65-66`), so the row belongs to the payee.
- On settlement that creator is paid, through `recordAppScopedPaymentEarnings` or `redeemableEarningsService.addEarnings({ userId: payment.user_id, source: "creator_revenue_share" })` (`x402-payment-requests.ts:809-814`). The payer is only recorded as a wallet in `metadata.payer` (`:800`).
- `refundAsCloudCredits` treats every `metadata.kind === "x402_payment_request"` row as the x402 rail (`crypto-payment-refunds.ts:81`). It requires `payment.organization_id === input.organizationId` (`:143-149`) and credits that organization (`:203`).
- x402 top-ups never create `crypto_payments` rows (`topup-handler.ts` writes the ledger directly), so every x402 row this service can reach is a payment request.

**Result:** an operator refund of an x402 payment request gives the payee Cloud credits on top of the earnings they already received, and the payer gets nothing. The existing test pinned this behavior: *"x402 payments refund as credits to the owning organization only"* refunds the x402 row to its owner org and expects success.

# Change

`refundAsCloudCredits` refuses the x402 rail with a new typed error, `CRYPTO_REFUND_X402_PAYER_UNBOUND`.

- The check runs after the destination-policy check, so on-chain and fiat requests keep their existing error, and before any refund-state read or ledger write.
- Crypto (wallet and OxaPay) refunds are unchanged.
- `CRYPTO_REFUND_POLICY` and the top-up refund statement are unchanged. Refunds are still credits-only; x402 payment requests simply have no organization to credit yet.
- The service header and the operator script's doc describe the refusal.
- The now-unreachable x402 branch of the refund description is removed.

This is deliberately fail-closed. Refunding an x402 payer properly needs a payer-to-organization binding and a reversal of the creator's earnings in the same transaction. That is a product decision under #22968, not something this PR invents.

# Testing

- [x] Targets `develop` and has no conflicts with the latest `origin/develop` (`4573712ebf`).
- [ ] Ran `bun install` and `bun run verify` after syncing; record blockers below.
- [ ] Updated relevant documentation.

This used Ubuntu 24.04 (WSL2), Bun 1.4.2 and `bun install --frozen-lockfile` on develop `4573712ebf`.

`crypto-payment-refunds.pglite.test.ts` exercises the real refund service and credit ledger on PGlite. The old x402 test is replaced by *x402 payment requests are refused: the record belongs to the payee, not the payer*. For both the owning (payee) org and another org, the refund rejects with `CRYPTO_REFUND_X402_PAYER_UNBOUND`, no `credit_transactions` row is written, and the payee's balance stays 0.

## Known gaps / failures

- `bun run verify` was not run in full on this host.
- Operators lose the (incorrect) x402 payment-request refund path until a payer binding exists. A payer who needs a refund must be handled manually, as for any payment outside this service.
- The review's non-blocking note stands: a confirmed OxaPay or wallet top-up has already granted `credits_to_add`, so a Cloud-credit refund of it adds credits on top. That needs its own policy decision and is not addressed here.

## Deployment

None. No migration or configuration change.
